#### Deployment Log v2.32
**Files deployed:** **1 executable surface (`core/deploy/deploy.sh`, +289 net) + a 13-file citation-correction surface + 4 rebuilt skill packages (+ their sha256 sidecars) + the release plan** — a skill-registry identity-and-currency slice making the already-shipped registry (`core/skills/registry.md`, ADR-038) self-checking against the deployed roster, plus closing the phantom-citation consumer surface. By deliverable: **(#1811 — row-currency, always-enforce)** `deploy.sh --check` gains `Check 5(d): Registry-currency (rows)`, appended after Check 5(c) and reusing Check 5's roster machinery, asserting three things against the deployed roster (`OPERATIONS_SKILLS + RELEASE_SKILLS + CORE_SKILLS`, `CANARY_SKILLS` excluded per ADR-04): (i) every registry row name ∈ roster; (ii) every roster member ∈ registry rows — FAIL on asymmetry in both directions; (iii) every registry row name resolves to a live `SKILL.md`. The row layer is structural / zero-FP, so findings increment `ISSUES` directly; it inherits the `cmd_check` `set +e` body + `set -u` empty-array guards and degrades a broken parse to a single loud audit-baseline FAIL rather than a fail-storm. **(#1658 — field-currency, warn-mode-initial)** a per-row inner assertion extends the row loop at a single marked nesting seam (so both layers share one roster source and cannot fork): for each role-Specialist row it checks recorded modes/dependencies (set-equality) + trigger-surface presence against the live `SKILL.md`, emitting `WARN` without gating until `registry-field-currency.mode` is flipped to `enforce` (per the established Check 14/15/18/19 shakedown precedent); a companion fix (`6e74427`) reconciled the `pmo-architect` composition set — frontmatter + its registry row — to its true 2-skill set so the field layer reads a clean row on a pristine roster. **(#1211 — consumer re-point)** 13 files across `core/`, `operations/`, `release/` that cited a phantom `dependency-graph.md` (a file that never shipped) now cite the live `core/skills/registry.md` (`2540cf2`); one net-new `registry.md` citation in `core/standards/skill-workspace-location.md` is de-linked to avoid a forward-reference (`996992e`) — pure reference correction, no behavior change. **(release-cut)** 4 drifted `.skill` packages (`build-reviewer`, `pmo-architect`, `pmo-skill-editor`, `pmo-skill-refiner`) rebuilt at release-cut (`13d90b4`). The shared `Check 5(d)` design ships as a design-note in the committed release plan (no ADR), per the Stage 5 scope-lock.
**Mechanism:** git merge (PR #2249 → main, `--merge`, merge SHA `42f029553574e8c69e4e795e68b65b10829f856c`) + signed-annotated `v2.32` tag at the merge SHA ("Good signature").
**Version note:** planned provisional at the `routine` class on the theme-named branch `release/v2.32-skill-registry-currency` (defer-to-merge; the durable declaration is the bump-class, not a number); the concrete number bound to **v2.32** at the Stage 12 atomic claim above the shipped anchor v2.31 (v2.32 free at claim time). The 4 consumption `.skill` packages were rebuilt at release-cut on the integration branch; the `.version` stamp + CHANGELOG append + the `chore/v2.32-stage-13-closeout` branch are the manual completions the automated close-out tool drops for theme-named (non-version-named) milestones — addressed in THIS release's Stage 13 close-out (see § Remaining hygiene).
**Timestamp:** 2026-06-27 (PR #2249 merged 21:57 UTC; Saturday).
**Cycle-Time:** N/A — no plan-review-go event recorded in the pipeline event log; mechanism: compute-cycle-time.sh.
**Result:** SUCCESS — PR #2249 merged with green CI (every gate `success`; the lone `Draft-file gate` showed a GitHub Checks-API status-cache race — `conclusion: success` + `completed_at` set, `status` stuck `in_progress` — with `mergeStateStatus: CLEAN`); signed `v2.32` tag pushed at `42f0295` ("Good signature"); GitHub Release published + marked Latest with the frontmatter-stripped note body. Post-deploy `deploy.sh --check`: `Check 5(d)` clean in BOTH layers — `OK: registry catalog current — 43 rows <-> roster (symmetric; canary excluded), every row resolves to a live SKILL.md; field-currency asserted per role-Specialist row (… mode=warn)`. `deploy.sh --deploy` (incremental) cleared the drift for the 5 v2.32-touched skills (build-reviewer, pmo-architect, pmo-skill-editor, pmo-skill-refiner, pmo-skill-refiner-selftest-canary); the single remaining hard FAIL is the pre-existing, out-of-scope `pmo-knowledge-manager — D-Refs threshold crossed` (NOT a v2.32 defect). Reversibility **CHEAP** / HIGH — additive build check + corrected citations; no data migration, no stored-content move, no breaking change; whole-release rollback = `git revert -m 1` of PR #2249 (skill edits additionally revert via the skill-edit path + package rebuild).
**Velocity:** 3 build issues (#1811 row-currency, #1658 field-currency, #1211 consumer re-point); class `routine`; files-changed = `deploy.sh` (+289 net) + 13 citation-correction files + 2 skill source files (registry.md, pmo-architect/SKILL.md) + 4 skill packages + 4 sha256 sidecars + the release plan.
**Outcome:** SUCCESS — finalized at Stage 13 VERIFIED. Post-deploy main exhibits the AFTER state: the skill registry is self-checking against the deployed roster at the row level (always-enforce, both-direction asymmetry FAIL + resolve-to-live-SKILL.md), with a field-currency inner layer logging per-row drift in warn-mode pending its shakedown flip; consumers that cited the phantom `dependency-graph.md` now read the live `registry.md`. #### Release Learnings v2.22
**Surprise:** the milestone entered Stage 4 recorded version-less, but at Stage 5 Collective Review the version-less posture was reconsidered and the release settled on **v2.22 (minor)** — all three cards make material edits to 5 skills whose `version:` field MUST bump to a release tag per `version-field-semantics.md` (a milestone slug is regex-invalid against `^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+(-[a-z]+)?$`), so there was no valid version-less provision once the skill-bump surface was in scope.
**Would-change:** surface the skill-`version:`-bump implication at the Stage 4 D-Version gate, not at Stage 5 — the deciding fact (5 skills bump, therefore a tag is mandatory) was knowable from the File Change Matrix at planning time; pulling it forward would have settled the version one stage earlier.
**Watch-for:** the **cite-by-path** single-sourcing choice (comms-writer Types 2/3 + `channel-formats.md` + the `MTG-###` field all point at the two new canonical specs rather than carrying inline copies) voids the duplicate-source-discipline drift risk by construction — but it means any future edit to the agenda/recap element set must land in the canonical spec only; an editor who "fixes" the format inline in a consuming skill re-introduces the very duplication this release removed. Also watch the **#56 decompose**: the foundation ships one domain (Estimation) seeded with its consuming skill — the 9 deferred #1173 domains must each preserve that seed-with-consumer floor to avoid an orphaned-corpus maintenance-debt class.

#### Deployment Log release-version-stamping
**Files deployed:** No runtime-skill or harness file is deployed by this release's own mechanism — the changed set is **release-tooling + governance/reference-corpus + one regression test + one CI-workflow wiring + the `.version` source file**, with **no `SKILL.md` and no `packages/` touched** (deploy no-op; the only mutated runtime-adjacent surfaces are `core/deploy/deploy.sh` and `release/tools/automated-closeout.sh`, which are scripts the repo ships, not deployed skill mirrors). Single release PR #1693 (`fix/version-stamp-ownership → main`, 8 files) closes #1643 — the `.version` source-of-truth was frozen at `v2.08` inside the `v2.09`/`v2.10` tags, so the `notify-version-skew.sh` SessionStart hook reported a perpetual "update available" that no `git pull`/`update.sh` could clear (the stale value is committed at the source; `update.sh` only re-propagates it per ADR-017). The 3-layer fix: **(L1)** corrects the stale value (now tracks the shipped release line on `main`); **(L2)** makes the `.version` stamp an owned Stage 13 close-out step — a new `phase_bump_version` in `release/tools/automated-closeout.sh` (with dry-run/PR-body wiring and `.version` added to the chore-commit staging list) plus declaring `.version` in the `release/references/pipeline/stage-13-close.md` output set + Phase B mechanism; **(L3)** adds `core/deploy/deploy.sh` **Check 39** asserting `.version` matches the latest reachable release tag (FAILs ≥2 minors behind, tolerates the legitimate 1-version Stage 12→13 window as WARN), with regression coverage in `core/deploy/tests/test_version_stamping.sh` wired into the suite via `.github/workflows/install-tests.yml`, and a `docs/UPDATE.md` clarification that `.version` is release-cut-owned. **Version-less identity:** milestone `release-version-stamping` (Release Class A — Routine) ships version-less per operator decision (Stage 4, 2026-06-20) following the `public-flip-install-blockers` / `intake-elicitation-skill` / `domain-aware-stage5-design` / `memory-to-corpus-codification` / `cross-reference-integrity-ci` precedents — no `vX.Y` assigned to this release, no signed-annotated tag cut, and no GitHub Release. Recorded explicitly: the tag-cut and Surface 1 (GitHub Releases) emit steps are deliberately omitted and their absence is not a close defect (version-less path; approved deviation, not omission). Corpus close-out (RELEASE_LOG `DEPLOYED → VERIFIED` + INDEX + DIGEST + RELEASE_NOTES) via this Stage 13 chore PR — hand-authored because `automated-closeout.sh` is version-keyed and cannot run for a version-less release (the documented Phase-B chore-PR mechanism).
**Mechanism:** git merge (PR #1693 → main), version-less, git-native [no skill deploy]. No skill-package rebuild and no `./core/deploy/deploy.sh --deploy` — the changed set carries no `{core,operations,release}/skills/` or `packages/` paths, so there is nothing to deploy or re-sync (deploy no-op; the merge activates Check 39 and the new regression on main). No tag (version-less).
**Timestamp:** 2026-06-20 UTC (PR #1693 merged 2026-06-21T01:22:51Z UTC at merge commit `1d1c0ec2ed3475be4403f5d606fd5a05f7501e11`; no tag — version-less).
**Cycle-Time:** N/A — version-less release (no deployment-status/deploy-skill or deploy-harness event in the pipeline event log; git-native release); mechanism: compute-cycle-time.sh.
**Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #1693 merged to main via merge commit `1d1c0ec2ed3475be4403f5d606fd5a05f7501e11` (D-C SINGLE; `git revert -m 1` rollback convention); no tag (version-less). Stage 7 Dev Testing PASS and Stage 8 QA ACCEPT — #1643's acceptance criteria verified, including the Check 39 WARN-vs-FAIL boundary calibration (no false-fail against the legitimate 1-version Stage 12→13 window) and the governance-edit-pairs-with-code-edit + new-phase-idempotency checks. **Issue #1643 stays OPEN at merge** (VERIFY-ONLY: PR #1693 used `References #1643` in its Issue References block — `closingIssuesReferences` empty — so the issue is closed manually at Stage 13 with per-AC verification evidence).
**Outcome:** Finalized at Stage 13 VERIFIED. Post-deploy main exhibits the AFTER state: `.version` tracks the shipped release line, `automated-closeout.sh` owns the `.version` bump at close, `stage-13-close.md` declares `.version` in the Stage 13 output set, and `deploy.sh` Check 39 guards against `.version` drifting behind the latest reachable release tag. Release Class **A (Routine)**, CHEAP reversibility / HIGH confidence (single `git revert -m 1` of the release PR; the L2/L3 additions are additive close-out tooling + an additive warn-mode-tolerant check, no data migration, deploy no-op means nothing to re-sync post-revert). **Surface 1 (GitHub Release) N/A** — version-less release (no version integer to tag or publish).

provenance = the *upstream human evidence* an artifact derives from, with `source_inputs` scoped out as a separate provenance concept not yet in-schema), records the **field-vs-verb composition note** (the scalars compose against the 7 MVP `relationships[]` verbs rather than extending them — no new enum value, the 7-MVP-type constraint unchanged), and specifies **sidecar `.meta.yml` parity** (the lineage fields carry identically on non-markdown artifacts so edges stay grep-discoverable) plus a **WARN-not-BLOCK dangling-lineage rule** (a lineage path that does not resolve is a flag, consistent with relationship-target resolution); syncs `core/standards/lifecycle-states-canonical.md §3.2` (the Artifact Workflow `DRAFT → REVIEWED → APPROVED → PROMOTED → ARCHIVED` restatement gains the three lineage-graph fields, with the schema-home cross-pointer to `frontmatter-schema.md`); and reconciles `core/standards/template-protocol.md` §9 (`topic` → `sibling_topic` — the third reconcile file caught at Stage 5, see FINDING below). **#1165 (artifact-lint — NEW skill):** authors `operations/skills/artifact-lint/SKILL.md`, a NEW Autonomy-Tier-1 **recommend-only** skill that scans a project's generated-artifact surface (`08-Generated/` + promoted folders) and runs **five graph-integrity checks — orphan / sibling-duplicate / stale-draft / displaced-content / version-chain** — reading the #334 lineage frontmatter, staging a single report to `08-Generated/artifact-lint-YYYY-MM-DD.md` with per-finding proposed action + reversibility tier + confidence + cited evidence, and performing **no file moves and no deletes** (the operator approves every action); ships `operations/skills/artifact-lint/references/work-plan-taxonomy.md` (the **17-row taxonomy** — Canonical-10 ≡ the Domain-C synthesis `artifact_type` set + Situational-7 ≡ the plan-type set + waterfall/cutover rows — that the displaced-content Check 4 uses as the canonical-target-folder lookup) and `references/edge-cases.md`; registered in `core/deploy/deploy.sh` `OPERATIONS_SKILLS` (and the `build-skill-packages.sh` `ALL_SKILLS` roster), shipping its own `.skill` package + `.sha256` content-hash baseline (`packages/artifact-lint.skill` + `.skill.sha256`); the per-skill output contract added to `core/schemas/per-skill-output-contracts.md` (4-section staged report). **FINDING-1 (dual state-read — the load-bearing input rule):** the stale-draft and version-chain checks read **`artifact_state` primary → `lifecycle_state` fallback**, because `artifact_state` (the §3.2 Artifact Workflow enum stamped by `artifact-generator`) is absent from `frontmatter-schema.md` while `lifecycle_state` is the schema's REQUIRED field — keying on one silently mis-reads every artifact carrying the other (false-negative stale drafts; phantom version-chain heads). **FINDING-2 (17-row taxonomy provenance):** no literal 17-row taxonomy survived in the canonical corpus (the original artifact-lineage-graph split card was deleted), so the taxonomy is an **evidence-grounded reconstruction** (Canonical-10 from the Domain-C `artifact_type` value set + Situational-7 from the plan-type set), **operator-confirmed at Stage 9 Collective Review** as the authoritative work-plan taxonomy — so Check 4 treats a canonical-home mismatch as a firm (still recommend-only) finding. **Stage-5 reconcile catch:** Stage 5 surfaced a **third** #334 reconcile target (`template-protocol.md` §9, `topic`→`sibling_topic`) beyond the two named at planning. **Follow-ups (forward-binding, not closed):** `core/artifact-workflow-protocol.md` is a planned co-located protocol doc — when it ships, `lifecycle-states-canonical.md §3.2`'s authoritative-source pointer updates to cross-reference it as the operational-protocol home (handled at that release's Stage 13). #### Deployment Log v1.24
**Files deployed:** No runtime-skill *or* harness file is deployed by this release's own mechanism — the changed set is **two K1/reference docs (`release/references/pipeline/stage-02-triage.md`, `release/governance/release-process.md`) + one CI-gate allowlist (`core/hooks/reference-durability-allowlist.txt`)**, with **no `SKILL.md`, no `packages/`, and no `core/rules/` mirror or hook logic touched** (deploy no-op — no `./core/deploy/deploy.sh --deploy` skill-mirror sync required; mirrors the v1.22 text/tool-only profile). The release (single PR #1068, single-issue `routine` release, single-branch D-C SINGLE) resolves the three v1.02-triage carry-forward findings on issue #309. **CF-2 (awk silent-zero):** the Stage-2 Triage A6.5 management-task detection table in `stage-02-triage.md` used `awk '/^### Dependencies/,/^### /'` (and the parallel `/^### Priority/,/^### /`) — a non-functional range, because the start regex is itself matched by the end regex `^### `, collapsing the range to the heading line so the body is dropped and the downstream `grep` returns nothing. All **3 affected instances** — Pattern 1b orphaned-dependency extraction, Pattern 2a P1-blocked-by-lower dependency extraction, Pattern 2a blocker-priority extraction — were replaced with the robust `awk 'BEGIN{flag=0} /^### <heading>/{flag=1;next} /^### /{flag=0} flag'` form (table-cell pipe-escaping preserved; Pattern 4a — a `grep -cE`, no awk — correctly untouched, which also corrected the issue body's stale "1b/2a/4a" instance inventory). **CF-3 (citation drift):** the Stage-2 Triage-cadence paragraph in `release-process.md` cited the sister management-task spec's "Stale issues" detection at `Phase A4 (ii) detection-pattern (1a)` — a stale anchor (the pattern lives at `Phase A6.5 Pattern (1a)`; Phase A4 there is the unrelated feasibility quick-check) — corrected to `Phase A6.5 Pattern (1a)` (the underlying P1-SLA bilateral contract is intact; only the anchor was wrong). **CF-1 (yq toolchain) DE-SCOPED:** the `yq` dependency targeted the **G3-13 Roadmap-Cascade Validation** detection bash in `core/schemas/gate-criteria-spec.md`, which ADR-012 (roadmap-instance-descope, Accepted 2026-06-02) already retired — no live detection bash remains carrying the dependency, so CF-1 was evidence-closed at verification, not engineered. **Three CI-gate remediations rode along** on the touched lines: the operator-handle reference on the re-touched A6.5 lines was tokenized, an `allow-link` marker was added (per-file reference-durability), and a `stage-02-triage.md` entry was added to `core/hooks/reference-durability-allowlist.txt` so the issue-ref detector does not false-positive on two pre-existing `#`-shaped patterns the awk-fix + tokenization re-surfaced in the added-lines delta (the `#5-process` anchor fragment of a `[Phase B B3](#5-process)` link, and the `#[0-9]+` grep regex inside the detection bash — an anchor fragment and a regex, NOT net-new fragile refs; mirrors the sibling stage-03 / stage-04 / stage-12 allowlist entries). Corpus close-out (RELEASE_NOTES + LOG `DEPLOYED → VERIFIED` + INDEX + DIGEST + CHANGELOG) lands at the Stage 13 chore PR.

No skill-package rebuild and no `./core/deploy/deploy.sh --deploy` — the changed set carries no `skills/`, `packages/`, or harness paths, so there is nothing to deploy or re-sync (deploy no-op). **Re-versioned planned v1.21 → v1.24:** authored/branched/PR-titled as `v1.21` (`release/v1.21-pipeline-detection-bash-debt`); `v1.21`/`v1.22`/`v1.23` were each claimed by concurrent releases (governance-as-code-quality-gates = v1.21, deploy-toolchain-defect-cleanup = v1.22, pmo-skill-reference-substrate = v1.23) between this release's planning and execute, so it landed at next-free **v1.24** (the #769 version-collision class). The release narrows the two PMO authoring skills to their PMO-unique surface, gives them an Anthropic-voice offload path that runs at design time (no runtime Anthropic dependency), and resolves a duplicate-ADR collision. **#174 (narrow comms-writer to 6 PMO-unique primary types):** `comms-writer` SKILL.md narrowed to the 6 communication types that are genuinely PMO-unique, with the PMO-critical rules (audience calibration, escalation discipline, status-theater guardrails) preserved rather than dropped in the narrowing. **#173 (comms-writer owns exec-brief + stakeholder-email — own-with-harvest):** `comms-writer` takes ownership of executive-brief and stakeholder-email generation under the **own-with-harvest** pattern — the Anthropic voice is harvested into the skill at design time, so generation carries no runtime Anthropic dependency. **#176 (narrow artifact-generator catalog — Part A+B):** the `artifact-generator` catalog narrowed to its PMO-unique artifact set (the catalog now enumerates 27 artifacts after the narrowing + the own-with-harvest additions), removing the commodity artifacts that an Anthropic scaffolding skill produces better. **#175 (artifact-generator Anthropic offload Wrapper Mode + Part-D routing tree — Part C+D):** a NEW **Wrapper Mode** in `artifact-generator` that offloads commodity artifact scaffolding to an Anthropic skill, plus a **Part-D routing decision tree** in `CLAUDE.md` that routes an artifact request to the PMO catalog vs. the Anthropic offload path. **#791 (resolve duplicate ADR-022 collision):** the duplicate `ADR-022` collision in `core/ADRs/` is resolved by renumbering the skill-sourcing-coupling-posture ADR to **`ADR-023-skill-sourcing-coupling-posture.md`** (the platform-config-vs-operator-toml-split ADR retains `ADR-022`), with the cross-references reconciled and the skill-sourcing registry trigger-(d) wired. Post-deploy main exhibits the AFTER state: `comms-writer` covers the 6 PMO-unique communication types and owns exec-brief + stakeholder-email generation via the own-with-harvest pattern (Anthropic voice harvested at design time, no runtime Anthropic dependency); `artifact-generator` enumerates a 27-artifact PMO-unique catalog and offloads commodity scaffolding through its new Wrapper Mode, with a Part-D routing decision tree in `CLAUDE.md` directing an artifact request to the PMO catalog vs. the Anthropic offload path; and the duplicate-`ADR-022` collision is resolved (`ADR-023-skill-sourcing-coupling-posture.md` + reconciled cross-references + registry trigger-(d)). `novel` release class, CHEAP reversibility (single `git revert -m 1` of the release PR — skill-scope narrowing + offload wrapper + routing tree + ADR renumber; no data migration; re-run `./deploy.sh --deploy` post-revert to re-sync skill mirrors), 30-day Stage 13 outcome window.
**Learnings:** (1) **In-pipeline re-version under concurrent releases** — this release was authored as `v1.15` but two concurrent releases (`platform-self-measurement-and-quality-method` as v1.15, `delivery-capacity-and-lifecycle-gating` as v1.16) claimed the lower numbers first, forcing a re-version to `v1.17`; the reusable lesson (already filed as #769 from v1.13) is that the Stage 12 / Phase A6.5 pre-merge check must re-verify the version number is still unclaimed, not just file/gate divergence. (2) **In-release ADR-collision resolution** — a duplicate `ADR-022` basename existed across two ADRs; resolving it in-release by renumbering the later-authored ADR to `ADR-023` (with cross-reference + registry reconciliation) is cleaner than shipping the collision and filing a fast-follow, because an ADR number is a durable reference other docs cite.

#### Deployment Log v1.16
**Files deployed:** Four NEW reference docs under `operations/skills/delivery-engine/references/` (`capacity-model.md`, `estimation-standards.md`, `lifecycle-stages.md`, `gate-definitions.md`) + the `delivery-engine` SKILL.md mode wiring (Modes C/D/E/F/G consume the new docs) + a surgical single-source edit to `operations/skills/delivery-engine/references/sprint-defaults.md` + the rebuilt `packages/delivery-engine.skill` package, all via release PR #859 merge (4-issue `novel` release; D-C SINGLE; sequenced #272 → #273 → #274 → #275). The release turns the delivery-engine's sprint-planning + release-readiness surfaces from rules-of-thumb into governed method across capacity, estimation, lifecycle, and gates. **#272 (capacity-model.md):** a NEW reference modeling *effective* delivery-team capacity — the capacity formula (§1), context-switching penalties across concurrent projects (§3), Brooks's-Law late-addition thresholds (§4), the 60/20/20 effort-allocation split (§5), team-stability + vendor-ramp thresholds (§6–§7), staffing-concentration / bus-factor risk (§8), and the demand-versus-supply gap RAG bands (§9); wired into `delivery-engine` Mode D (capacity formula + penalties) + Mode E (demand-supply gap). Two inputs are referenced not restated — the focus factor (owned by `estimation-standards.md` §3) and the platform bus-factor rule (owned by `core/disciplines/km-protocols.md` §4). **#273 (estimation-standards.md):** a NEW reference, the canonical home for estimation discipline — Cone-of-Uncertainty band widths bounding precision by lifecycle phase (§1, normative cone rule rejects an over-tight range), planning-horizon commit-vs-forecast rules, the **canonical focus-factor table this doc now solely owns** (§3, promoted unchanged from its sole prior occurrence in `sprint-defaults.md` §1.2 — the single-source move), the three-zone buffer model, the velocity-and-estimates-as-ranges enforcement rule (§5), and the contingency-vs-management-reserve distinction; wired into Mode D (bands + horizon + focus-factor table + buffer model) + Mode E (velocity-as-range enforcement). **#274 (lifecycle-stages.md):** a NEW reference enumerating the **15-stage universal delivery lifecycle** (the temporal axis), five-phase super-grouped (Capture → Prepare → Build → Validate → Deliver), with per-stage entry/exit criteria + consumed/produced artifacts and a 5-of-8 methodology mapping; wired into Mode C (execution-stage entry criteria) + Mode F (validation-stage exit criteria, incl. the QA/Acceptance exit predicate that blocks Plan Review while a P1 defect is open). Carries an explicit boundary statement (project delivery lifecycle ≠ the platform's own release pipeline). **#275 (gate-definitions.md):** a NEW reference, the canonical sequence of **eleven project-lifecycle gates** — `LG-0` (Idea Screen) through `LG-10` (Closure) — each with entry/exit criteria, key decisions, authority holder, required artifacts, and escalation path; wired into Mode F (per-gate exit criteria + transition rule) + Mode G (gate-decision attribution to gate + authority holder) + Mode C (situates sprint DoR as LG-4). Cross-references the sibling gate-TYPE taxonomy (`gate-checklists.md`) rather than restating it; the `LG-` prefix is deliberately namespaced to avoid collision with the release-pipeline's `G-PR*/G-EX*/G-CL*` stage gates. The three governance docs (`lifecycle-stages.md` temporal axis · `gate-definitions.md` gate-instance axis · `gate-checklists.md` gate-type axis) form a clean three-axis model, each owning one axis and cross-referencing the others. **Result:** SUCCESS — release PR #859 merged to main via merge commit `a915daa94caa68423b42cbf7e7db69a4dfbc37e1` (D-C SINGLE; `git revert -m 1` rollback convention); signed-annotated `v1.16` tag points at the merge SHA; zero signing bypasses; the rebuilt `delivery-engine.skill` package is staged from scrubbed source so package↔source parity (Check 7) holds on main. A concurrent v1.15 merged into main mid-pipeline; the resulting conflict on the `delivery-engine` SKILL.md was resolved at Stage 12 with both edit-sets (v1.15 RCA additions + this release's Mode C/D/E/F/G wiring) preserved. Two CI gates (durable-corpus `#N` fragility) flagged at Stage 9 Plan Review were de-fragiled before merge.

**Learnings:** (1) **Concurrent-release SKILL.md conflict** — a concurrent v1.15 merging into main mid-pipeline produced a same-file conflict on `delivery-engine/SKILL.md` (both releases edited it); the reusable lesson is that two in-flight releases touching the same skill SKILL.md must resolve at the later release's Stage 12 with BOTH edit-sets preserved (keep-both, not last-writer-wins), then rebuild the `.skill` package against the merged source so package↔source parity holds. (2) **Durable-corpus fragility caught at Stage 9** — two CI gates flagged a durable-corpus fragile-reference (`#N`) at Plan Review; the reusable lesson is that the durable-corpus fragile-reference gate fires on new reference docs that carry bare issue refs in prose, and de-fragiling (allow-link marker or by-name rewrite) belongs at Stage 9 before merge, not as a post-merge fast-follow.

#### Release Learnings v1.01

**Surprise:** R-A scope expansion adopted at Stage 5 Collective Review (initial scope was the Stage 12 spec only; expanded to scrub all 7 file locations carrying the prior lightweight tag form) — re-classified the release from `routine` to `cross-cutting`. Cheaper-to-stricter transition was correctly applied; per-action signatures already rendered remained valid.

**Would-change:** The `core/CLAUDE.md.template` tier-selection wording sync was logged as Tier 1 out-of-scope drift at Stage 5 (kept under cross-cutting (b) threshold) but emerged as a follow-up Issue at Stage 13. A more aggressive R-A scope expansion at Stage 5 (or a Stage 4 R-A scope audit gate) might have caught the template surface earlier. Net: deferral was correct under the threshold discipline; mechanism for future R-A scope audits could surface template/generator surfaces explicitly.

**Watch-for:** First downstream release after v1.01-intake will be the first consumer of (a) the G-PR8 mid-pipeline divergence check at Stage 9 Phase A6.5, (b) the AC-Drift Handling Protocol verdict enum at Stage 8, and (c) the new `Outcome:` line on the Deployment Log per #218 tracking. Both #105 and #274 carry explicit cutover clauses exempting v1.01-intake itself. If the first downstream release has a clean Stage 9 entry (no concurrent release mid-pipeline) and clean Stage 8 (no drifted ACs), G-PR8 + Drift-protocol firing remains unobserved — empirical validation deferred until a representative test case emerges in production. The reflexive-pipeline-loop discipline is now an established pattern across 3 cutover-clause subjects in a single release; reusable convention for future protocol-shipping releases.
